12. Q: How should travel advances be recorded in the general ledger when the money is paid to staff?
A: Show answer
Under a typical accrual-based arrangement, payment of an advance records an asset or receivable until the organization receives and approves evidence of eligible expenditure or otherwise settles the amount. An illustrative entry is debit Travel Advances and credit Cash or Bank when the advance is paid. The exact account names and subsidiary-ledger design depend on the organization's accounting system. In the UN Secretariat's Umoja travel process, advance postings use a travel-advance account and a traveller vendor account, illustrating how the subledger and general ledger can be integrated. That account structure should not be copied automatically by another organization. Finance should retain the trip reference, traveller identifier, amount, currency, payment date, funding code and outstanding status. Subsequent expense recognition and clearance should be supported by the approved report and settlement calculation.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare the initial advance journal and demonstrate how the balance remains identifiable for the individual traveller."

Key point:
- An advance normally remains a balance-sheet item until it is substantiated or settled.
- Account codes must follow the organization's approved chart of accounts.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel advance posting
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger postings
13. Q: When should travel expenses be recognized in the accounting records?
A: Show answer
Expenses should be recognized according to the applicable accounting framework and the point at which the relevant goods or services are consumed or the expense is otherwise incurred. Payment timing and claim-submission timing are not necessarily the recognition dates. Airfare purchased directly by the organization may require treatment different from an advance paid to a traveller or a service that has not yet been used. A trip completed before period-end can create an expense even if the final settlement is received later. The UN IPSAS Finance Manual's travel-process guidance notes that the Umoja posting may occur when a claim is approved, while the accounting expense may relate to the date of travel. Finance should therefore assess period-end cut-off, substantiation and estimates rather than allowing system-processing dates alone to determine recognition. The specific accounting treatment should be confirmed under the organization's adopted framework and policies.

Study instruction:
- "Compare the payment date, travel date, claim approval date and ledger posting date for one trip and explain the appropriate accounting cut-off."

Key point:
- Accounting recognition is not necessarily determined by the date cash is paid or the claim is processed.
- System posting rules must be reconciled with the applicable accounting framework.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel advance and expense recognition
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and period-end accounting
14. Q: How should expense settlements be calculated when actual eligible expenditure differs from the advance?
A: Show answer
Calculate the final eligible expenditure using the approved method for each category, including per diem, transportation, accommodation and permitted incidental costs. Compare the resulting total with all advances or other trip payments already made. If eligible expenditure is higher than the advance, the organization may owe an additional reimbursement after approval. If eligible expenditure is lower, the unused amount remains recoverable until the traveller returns it or another permitted settlement method clears the balance. The calculation should separately identify rejected costs, amounts already paid directly by the organization, currency conversion where applicable and any adjustment to the project allocation. The resulting balance must agree with the settlement journal and the individual advance ledger. It should not be closed merely because the expense claim has reached an approved status if a refund, repayment or additional payment is still outstanding.

Study instruction:
- "Reconcile the approved advance to actual eligible costs and prepare both an amount-due-to-traveller case and an amount-recoverable-from-traveller case."

Key point:
- Final settlement equals approved eligible costs compared with the advances and other payments already made.
- Residual amounts must be paid or recovered and supported by evidence.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel claim settlement and postings
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Journal entries and reconciliation
15. Q: How should unused travel advances be returned and recorded?
A: Show answer
An unused balance should be calculated from the approved settlement and returned through an authorized payment channel or another formally permitted recovery process. Finance should issue clear payment instructions, identify the trip reference and the amount owed, and set a follow-up date. After receiving the funds, the accountant should verify the bank or cash receipt, match it to the correct traveller and trip, post the appropriate clearing entry and update the advance register. If the refund is paid in a different currency, apply the organization's approved foreign-exchange policy and explain any difference. A promise to refund is not evidence that cash was received, and a bank deposit must not be allocated to an advance solely because the amount looks similar. Any remaining balance should stay open until verified evidence supports closure or an authorized alternative resolution is completed.

Study instruction:
- "Trace one unused advance from the settlement calculation through the refund instruction, bank receipt, journal and closing balance."

Key point:
- A refund is complete only when the cash receipt and accounting clearance are supported.
- Unresolved balances must remain visible and assigned for follow-up.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel advance recovery and settlement
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and account clearing
16. Q: How should additional amounts owed to a traveller be paid after an approved settlement?
A: Show answer
When eligible expenses exceed the advance, Finance should prepare a settlement calculation showing the approved expenditure, initial advance, other amounts already paid and net amount due. The calculation must be independently checked and approved under the applicable authority limits. Before payment, the payment operator should verify the traveller's identity, approved amount, currency, destination account or other permitted payment details, and any required tax or payroll handling. The payment reference should link to the original trip and approved claim. The transaction should be recorded consistently with the travel expense and advance-clearing entries, preventing a second expense from being recognized simply because an additional payment is made. Where the claim is disputed or incomplete, only the amount permitted by policy should proceed, with the remainder documented as unresolved.

Study instruction:
- "Reconcile a settlement that produces a reimbursement due and trace the approved net amount to the final payment record."

Key point:
- Additional reimbursement must be calculated from approved expenses less amounts already funded.
- Paying a balance should clear the settlement, not create duplicate expense recognition.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel claims and payment processing
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and payment controls
17. Q: How should overdue travel advances be monitored and escalated?
A: Show answer
Maintain an advance register with the traveller, trip reference, issue date, authorized return date, amount, currency, expected settlement date, actual return date, outstanding balance and status. Produce an aging report based on approved internal deadlines, separating advances not yet due from overdue items and material exceptions. Send reminders before the deadline and escalate unresolved items to the traveler's supervisor and Finance Manager according to a documented escalation matrix. The reviewer should consider whether the trip was completed, whether the employee is still active, whether the claim is blocked by missing evidence, and whether the balance is disputed. Do not assume that an overdue advance becomes an expense or should be written off. The organization must follow its approved recovery, payroll and legal procedures. Repeated overdue claims should be analyzed for root causes, such as unclear instructions or late expense-data submission.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare an aging report and assign a responsible person, due date and escalation route to each overdue advance."

Key point:
- Aging identifies balances requiring action and management attention.
- Overdue status does not eliminate the need for accounting evidence and approved recovery procedures.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel advance monitoring and recovery
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 13 — Receivables and outstanding balances
18. Q: What should happen if a traveller submits the expense report late or does not settle the advance?
A: Show answer
The SOP should establish the approved submission deadline, reminder sequence, escalation path, evidence of delivery and consequences permitted by organizational policy and applicable law. Finance should first confirm the trip's completion date, the amount still outstanding and whether the delay is caused by missing documents, illness, system problems or other valid circumstances. The traveller should receive a documented request for the claim or repayment. If the delay persists, escalate it to the responsible manager and follow the approved recovery procedure. Do not copy a deadline or automatic payroll-recovery mechanism from the UN Secretariat's Umoja manual and assume it applies to an independent NGO. Any deduction from salary must be reviewed for legal authority, contractual basis, required notices and applicable consent or payroll rules. Exceptions should be approved, time-limited and monitored to prevent old advances from remaining indefinitely unresolved.

Study instruction:
- "Simulate an overdue advance and document the reminder, response, escalation, recovery decision and evidence required for closure."

Key point:
- Late claims require timely follow-up and a documented resolution path.
- Payroll recovery and other collection measures must follow applicable law and policy.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel expense report submission and recovery process
- United Nations Policy Portal — Travel regulations and rules
19. Q: How should a travel advance be handled when a trip is cancelled, shortened, extended or materially changed?
A: Show answer
Finance should compare the original authorization with the actual travel undertaken and determine which costs have been incurred, which services are refundable and whether any additional payment is justified. For a cancelled trip, the traveller should not retain an advance merely because it was validly authorized before cancellation. Recover the unused amount and record any non-refundable costs under the applicable rules. For a shortened trip, recalculate any date-based allowance and identify unused funds. For an extension, obtain approval for additional dates and costs before issuing further funds whenever practicable. Changes in destination, transport, accommodation or funding may require additional approval and a revised risk assessment. Retain the original authorization, updated approval, cancellation notices, credit notes and settlement calculation so the changes are traceable and the same cost is not claimed twice.

Study instruction:
- "Reconcile the original budget, revised authorization, actual trip dates, supplier refunds and final travel settlement for a changed itinerary."

Key point:
- The final settlement must reflect the trip actually undertaken and eligible costs incurred.
- Changed travel arrangements should trigger documented reassessment and approval.

Source links:
- United Nations Policy Portal — Authorized travel dates, route and entitlements
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel request amendments and claim settlement
20. Q: How should foreign currency travel advances and expense claims be accounted for?
A: Show answer
Record the original transaction currency, amount, transaction date, settlement currency, exchange rate, rate source and approved conversion method. A traveller might receive an advance in one currency but incur eligible costs in another; the organization must follow its approved foreign-exchange and rounding policy to determine the reportable expense and any residual balance. The accounting treatment may differ between an advance measured at its initial rate, expense recognition at the relevant transaction date and any monetary balance outstanding at reporting date. Do not use an arbitrary month-end rate to recalculate all historical transactions without considering the applicable accounting policy. Retain exchange-rate evidence and make any exchange difference identifiable rather than hiding it within travel expenses. The amount recoverable from the traveller must also be clearly stated in an agreed currency and reconciled to the ledger.

Study instruction:
- "Recalculate a foreign-currency advance settlement and distinguish the original advance, actual expenditure, closing receivable and any exchange difference."

Key point:
- Currency conversion needs a documented and consistently applied method.
- Exchange differences must be distinguished from travel cost variances and recovery balances.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel advance and expense postings
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger accounting and reconciliation
21. Q: How should the travel advance and settlement process address expenses paid directly by the organization?
A: Show answer
The expense report must identify amounts paid directly to airlines, hotels, transport providers, conference organizers or other suppliers so the traveller does not claim them again. Finance should reconcile supplier invoices and payment records to the trip authorization and cost category. Where a supplier payment is already recorded in accounts payable or a prepaid-expense account, the settlement calculation should exclude any duplicate reimbursement while ensuring the expense is recognized in the correct period. The traveller may still be entitled to a separate per diem or local expense reimbursement if permitted by policy. Direct payments should be linked to the trip reference, supplier invoice, project code and approval. Exceptions such as cancelled hotel bookings, refunds and supplier credits require follow-up so that recoverable amounts are not overlooked.

Study instruction:
- "Compare the traveller's expense report with travel-agent invoices, hotel bills and payment records to identify duplicate or omitted costs."

Key point:
- Supplier-paid costs and traveller claims must reconcile to one approved trip cost.
- Refunds and credits should be tracked to completion.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel invoices and expense report postings
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 14 — Accounts Payable
22. Q: How should travel costs be allocated to projects, grants, donors and cost centres?
A: Show answer
Allocate travel costs according to the approved business purpose, the activity actually supported and the applicable accounting policy, project budget and donor agreement. Each expense line should carry the correct entity, project or grant code, cost centre, account, funding source and reporting period. A trip supporting multiple projects may require a reasonable allocation based on documented time, itinerary or another approved allocation basis. The chosen method must be consistent, explainable and compatible with the grant terms. Do not automatically charge the full advance to a project at payment date if final eligible costs are still unknown. At settlement, compare the approved allocation with actual costs and correct the coding through an authorized journal if necessary. Check that costs charged to donor-funded projects are eligible and are not also recovered from another funding source.

Study instruction:
- "Take a multi-project trip and demonstrate how its eligible costs are allocated, approved, posted and reconciled without duplicate donor charging."

Key point:
- Final project allocation should reflect eligible costs and the approved allocation basis.
- Donor-funded expenses must not be charged twice or contrary to grant conditions.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel cost assignments and postings
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger coding and control
23. Q: What is the difference between travel advance settlement and reimbursement of an employee's personal expenditure?
A: Show answer
An advance begins with funds paid by the organization before expenditure is fully substantiated. Settlement determines how much of those funds is supported by eligible costs and whether money must be returned or additional reimbursement paid. A reimbursement claim begins when an employee has already paid an eligible organizational expense personally and requests repayment. Both processes need authorization, eligibility checks, supporting evidence, approval and correct project coding, but their starting accounting balances differ. A reimbursement should not be treated as settlement of an advance unless the amounts are demonstrably related and the approved process permits that offset. Mixing the processes can result in an advance remaining open even when a related claim has been paid, or in costs being reimbursed twice. Separate reference fields should identify the original advance and all related settlement transactions.

Study instruction:
- "Compare a trip funded entirely by an advance with a trip financed by the employee's own funds and identify the different journal and settlement flows."

Key point:
- Advance settlement clears money previously provided by the organization.
- Reimbursement repays eligible expenditure already funded by the employee.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel advances and expense claims
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Journal entries and balances
24. Q: How should travel advances be reconciled with the general ledger and individual traveller records?
A: Show answer
Maintain a detailed register that records each advance by trip reference, traveller, amount, currency, transaction date, settlement status, refund or reimbursement and ledger reference. At each close, reconcile the total open-item register to the appropriate general ledger balance and subsidiary ledger. Investigate differences such as duplicate postings, missing refunds, claims posted to the wrong account, unallocated bank receipts, currency variances and settlements that were approved but not posted. Verify both the individual balances and the overall control-account total. Corrections should identify the original entry, reason, supporting evidence, preparer and reviewer. A balancing journal should not be used merely to force the register to agree with the ledger without explaining the underlying transactions. Material unexplained differences must remain open and be escalated before the reconciliation is certified.

Study instruction:
- "Reconcile a sample advance register to the general ledger, investigate each residual and document evidence of resolution."

Key point:
- The register and ledger must reconcile with an explainable audit trail.
- Unexplained differences require investigation rather than unsupported adjustments.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 13 — Accounts Receivable and advances
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and reconciliations
25. Q: How should outstanding travel advances be reviewed at month-end and year-end?
A: Show answer
Produce a complete list of advances outstanding at the reporting date, confirm whether each trip has started or finished, and determine what evidence exists for expenditure already incurred. Separate trips not yet undertaken from completed trips awaiting claims, partially settled trips and disputed or overdue balances. Under accrual accounting, eligible travel costs incurred before period-end may need to be recognized even when the final claim has not been submitted. Any required estimate should be based on available evidence, reasonable assumptions and the organization's accounting policy, with approval and a subsequent review when actual costs become known. The unspent portion of an advance should remain identifiable as a recoverable balance until properly cleared. The close file should contain the aging, cut-off assessment, relevant journals, reconciliations, unresolved items and review sign-off.

Study instruction:
- "Perform a reporting-date review of outstanding advances and distinguish unincurred travel, incurred but unreported expense and recoverable cash."

Key point:
- Reporting-date cut-off must reflect travel and expenses incurred, not only claim-processing status.
- Estimates and remaining advance balances require separate support and review.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Year-end travel advance procedures
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Accruals and period-end close
26. Q: What journal entries may be needed to record an advance, recognize eligible expenses and settle the final balance?
A: Show answer
The exact entries depend on the accounting framework and chart of accounts, but a typical sequence starts with a debit to Travel Advances and a credit to Bank when the advance is paid. On approval of eligible costs, Finance may debit the appropriate travel expense accounts and credit Travel Advances for the portion cleared. If the approved expense exceeds the advance, the excess may be recorded as a payable to the traveller and cleared when reimbursement is paid. If the advance exceeds eligible expenditure, the unutilized balance remains recoverable until a refund is received or another permitted recovery is completed. For example, if an advance is 1,000 monetary units and approved eligible costs are 850, 850 is cleared to expense and the remaining 150 is recoverable. The example is illustrative only; actual account names and posting timing must follow approved policy.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare journals for advance issuance, settlement, overpayment recovery and additional reimbursement, then reconcile every entry to the supporting evidence."

Key point:
- Advance, expense, payable and recovery balances have different accounting purposes.
- Each journal must be supported by an approved calculation and linked transaction reference.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel posting schemes
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger entries
27. Q: How should an expense claim with missing receipts or incomplete documentation be handled?
A: Show answer
Finance should identify the specific missing evidence, the amount affected, the relevant expense category and the policy requirement. The traveller should be asked to provide the missing document or an alternative form of evidence expressly permitted by policy. An alternative explanation should contain enough information to assess the business purpose, actual occurrence and amount; it should not be accepted automatically simply because a receipt is unavailable. The reviewer should determine whether the expense may be approved, needs further investigation or must be rejected under the governing rules. Material exceptions require documented approval by an authorized independent person. An incomplete line should not prevent the organization from settling unrelated, fully supported items where partial settlement is allowed. The unsupported amount must remain visible and be treated consistently until resolved.

Study instruction:
- "Review an incomplete claim and document the evidence requested, permitted alternative, decision authority and final disposition."

Key point:
- Missing documentation must trigger a defined review process.
- Unsupported costs must not be silently approved or concealed within a total.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel expense report review
- United Nations Policy Portal — Travel expense evidence and entitlements
28. Q: What controls help prevent duplicate travel claims and duplicate advance payments?
A: Show answer
Assign a unique trip identifier and preserve links between the travel request, advance, supplier invoices, expense report, refunds and settlement journals. Before processing a payment, check the traveller, trip dates, amount, currency, expense category, invoice or receipt reference and payment status. Duplicate detection should use multiple fields because different suppliers may issue identical invoice numbers and a single expense may appear in several systems. A suspected duplicate should be investigated rather than automatically deleted. The reviewer should determine whether it is a genuine duplicate, a legitimate split cost, a credit-note correction or a separate transaction. Retain the result of the investigation, including any approved exclusion or reversal. Compare transaction counts and total values before and after data transformations so automated processing does not remove valid expenses or double-count the same claim.

Study instruction:
- "Test an expense dataset containing deliberately duplicated lines and verify that the control identifies candidates without removing legitimate transactions."

Key point:
- Unique references and multi-field matching reduce duplicate payment and expense risk.
- Candidate duplicates require review and a recorded decision.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel transaction processing
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger control and audit trail
29. Q: How should unusual travel expenses, policy breaches and suspected misuse be investigated?
A: Show answer
Establish a documented exception process covering unusually high costs, repeated claims for the same expense, personal expenditure presented as official, altered receipts, duplicate travel, unexplained itinerary changes, advances split to avoid approval limits and repeated late settlements. First preserve the relevant authorization, claim, payment and system history. Compare the transaction with policy, actual itinerary, approved budget and available source evidence. Obtain explanations from the responsible individuals and involve an independent reviewer where appropriate. Do not characterize an exception as fraud solely because it is unusual; assess the evidence objectively and escalate suspected misconduct under the organization's reporting and investigation procedures. Record the outcome, recovery decision, financial correction and control improvement. Access to sensitive investigation information should be restricted to authorized personnel.

Study instruction:
- "Investigate a sample high-risk travel claim from original authorization to payment and document the evidence supporting the conclusion."

Key point:
- Exceptions need independent review, evidence preservation and proportionate escalation.
- A suspected breach must be investigated rather than assumed or dismissed.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel controls and reviews
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Financial control and audit trail
30. Q: How should the SOP address personal extensions, private travel and mixed business-personal itineraries?
A: Show answer
The policy should distinguish authorized business dates and costs from personal time and expenditure. Before the trip, the traveller should disclose a requested private extension and obtain any required authorization. The organization should establish the cost it would have incurred for the approved business itinerary and determine which incremental costs are personal and must be borne by the traveller. The comparison must follow the applicable policy and should not rely on an unsupported estimate of a hypothetical ticket price. Additional hotel nights, personal activities and deviations for convenience must be classified separately. Where personal arrangements change the booking or reimbursement, Finance should retain the approved itinerary, cost comparison and supporting invoices. The final expense report should exclude personal costs and clearly explain any permitted deviation.

Study instruction:
- "Compare an approved business itinerary with a proposed private extension and identify the costs that the organization and traveller should bear under policy."

Key point:
- Personal expenditure must be separated from eligible organizational travel costs.
- Deviations and incremental costs require the applicable advance approval and evidence.

Source links:
- United Nations Policy Portal — Authorized travel dates, routes and personal deviations
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel authorization and settlement
31. Q: How should travel advances be processed when the traveller leaves the organization or becomes unavailable?
A: Show answer
Identify all outstanding advances when an employee resigns, transfers, takes prolonged leave or otherwise becomes unavailable to complete settlement. Finance should confirm the last known trip status, identify claims not yet submitted, recoverable amounts and supporting evidence already available. HR, the line manager and Finance should coordinate responsibilities without assuming that an outstanding balance may automatically be deducted from final salary. Any collection or offset must follow the applicable contract, law, payroll rules and organizational authorization. If the employee cannot be contacted, record the attempts, escalation, legal or contractual assessment and approved next action. A balance should remain open until evidence supports settlement, lawful recovery or another formally approved accounting resolution. A write-off must not be used simply because the former employee is no longer active.

Study instruction:
- "Review an employee-exit case and trace each open travel advance through the handover, contact attempts, recovery assessment and approved resolution."

Key point:
- Staff departure requires a deliberate review of all open advances and claims.
- Recovery and write-off decisions must follow authorized procedures and applicable law.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 13 — Accounts Receivable and recovery
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and outstanding balances
32. Q: How should tax and payroll implications of travel allowances and reimbursements be assessed?
A: Show answer
The organization should assess the nature of each payment under the tax and payroll rules applicable to its legal entity, jurisdiction, personnel category and transaction. A payment described as per diem or reimbursement is not automatically exempt from tax or excluded from payroll reporting. Relevant factors may include the purpose of travel, actual substantiation, approved rates, contractual status, payment form, local reporting rules and whether the amount exceeds a permitted threshold. Finance should document the conclusion using applicable official legislation and guidance, and obtain qualified advice where the rules are unclear. The tax treatment should be kept separate from the internal accounting classification: a cost may be a travel expense in the ledger while also requiring payroll or tax reporting. Changes to rates or personnel arrangements should trigger a review of the approved treatment.

Study instruction:
- "Select representative domestic and international travel payments and identify which tax, payroll and reporting rules require confirmation before settlement."

Key point:
- The accounting label does not determine tax treatment.
- Applicable law and evidence must be reviewed before concluding that an allowance is exempt.

Source links:
- United Nations Policy Portal — Travel entitlements and allowances
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel expenses and posting
33. Q: How can Excel and Power Query support a controlled travel advance and expense settlement register?
A: Show answer
Excel can hold structured tables for traveller information, approved trips, advances, daily per diem calculations, expense lines, refunds, outstanding balances and settlement status. Power Query can combine payroll, general ledger, bank, booking and expense-report exports, standardize column names, convert dates and currencies into consistent fields, and identify candidate duplicates or missing references. Master tables can validate project codes, funding sources, expense categories and approval statuses. The design should preserve raw source records, record refresh dates and reconcile transaction counts and totals before processed data is used. Query transformations do not determine legal eligibility or replace independent review. The workbook should distinguish input, processing and output layers and include an exceptions report so incomplete or disputed transactions are not silently accepted as final.

Study instruction:
- "Build a structured advance register and use Power Query to combine sample source files while reconciling row counts, values and exceptions."

Key point:
- Structured data and repeatable transformations reduce manual rework.
- Automation must preserve source evidence and expose unresolved exceptions.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel process and settlement controls
- Microsoft Learn — What is Power Query?
34. Q: How can VBA automate reminders, settlement journals and travel exception reporting without weakening financial controls?
A: Show answer
VBA can generate a list of overdue advances, create settlement workpapers from approved data, assemble monthly reports, export controlled PDF outputs and record the batch reference and execution timestamp. A well-designed macro should not automatically approve a claim, determine a complex eligibility question or initiate a bank payment without the required authorization. Before generating a journal, it should verify that mandatory fields exist, the claim has an approved status, the supporting references are identifiable, the settlement calculation reconciles and no unresolved blocking exception remains. Error handling should stop the process safely, show a clear message and log failures rather than silently continuing with incomplete data. Changes to formulas, tax parameters or workflow rules should be tested on a controlled sample and approved before deployment. The output should retain traceability to its source transactions and the version of the workbook used.

Study instruction:
- "Test a VBA settlement workflow with missing references, unapproved claims and unbalanced journals, and confirm that each condition is blocked or escalated appropriately."

Key point:
- VBA is useful for routine workflow and reporting tasks, not for bypassing approval or accounting judgment.
- Error handling, testing, version control and audit logging are essential.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger controls
- Microsoft Learn — Excel VBA reference
35. Q: What controls should be used to protect the confidentiality and integrity of travel settlement records?
A: Show answer
Travel files can contain employee identifiers, bank details, itineraries, signatures, receipts and other sensitive personal information. Access should be limited to personnel who need the data for authorization, settlement, accounting, audit or other legitimate purposes. Store the records in approved repositories with appropriate permissions, access logging and backup arrangements. Do not include passwords, authentication tokens or unnecessary personal information in shared registers. Corrections should retain the original record, the reason for change, the person making it, the approval and the timestamp. Apply retention and disposal rules consistent with law, contractual obligations, donor requirements and organizational policy. Periodically review access permissions and test recovery of archived records. The organization should also ensure that any external travel agent or service provider receives only the information required to perform the authorized task.

Study instruction:
- "Review a sample travel archive for access restrictions, unnecessary personal data, version history, retention controls and recoverability."

Key point:
- Confidentiality and auditability must be maintained throughout the record lifecycle.
- Sensitive data should not be exposed in unrestricted workbooks or informal communications.

Source links:
- United Nations Policy Portal — Travel documentation and administrative controls
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Financial records and control
36. Q: What should the travel settlement archive contain so that an auditor can reconstruct an individual trip?
A: Show answer
The archive should connect the approved business purpose and budget to the payment, actual travel, eligible expenditure, review, settlement and final ledger entries. Depending on the trip, this includes the request and approval, itinerary, advance calculation, payment evidence, expense report, required receipts and invoices, per diem schedule, supporting explanations, project allocation, reviewer sign-off, refund or reimbursement record and accounting references. Keep a clear version history where records are amended and identify unresolved exceptions separately. The archive should be searchable by trip reference, traveller, date, project and payment reference without requiring access to unrelated personnel records. Retention periods and permitted forms of evidence must follow the organization's approved policy and applicable legal or donor requirements. Audit readiness depends on the completeness and traceability of the file, not merely on the fact that a PDF has been saved.

Study instruction:
- "Select one settled trip and demonstrate that every material amount can be traced from authorization to source evidence, payment, settlement and general ledger."

Key point:
- The archive must establish a complete audit trail from request to final reconciliation.
- Retention and evidence requirements must follow applicable policy and law.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel transaction lifecycle
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and supporting records
37. Q: How should management measure whether the staff travel advance and settlement process is effective?
A: Show answer
Define a small set of measurable indicators supported by the travel register and accounting records. Useful measures may include the proportion of claims submitted by the internal deadline, average settlement time after trip completion, number and value of overdue advances, age of unresolved balances, percentage of settlements requiring correction, number of duplicate-payment exceptions and the value of unexplained reconciliation differences. Each metric should have a clear numerator, denominator, reporting frequency, owner, target and escalation threshold. For example, timely settlement should be measured against all claims due in the period, not only those successfully closed. Management should review trends across multiple periods and investigate recurring delays by cause, such as incomplete documentation, approval bottlenecks or unreliable source data. Automated dashboards should be reconciled to the underlying register before they are used for management decisions.

Study instruction:
- "Design a monthly dashboard with reproducible KPI definitions and reconcile every reported measure to the underlying travel and settlement records."

Key point:
- KPIs should measure both process timeliness and the quality of financial control.
- Dashboards require verified data and defined escalation thresholds.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel management and settlement monitoring
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and financial reporting controls
38. Q: How should the SOP be reviewed and updated when travel policies, donor requirements or accounting rules change?
A: Show answer
Assign a policy owner to monitor relevant official guidance, approved internal policy revisions, donor agreements, delegated authorities and accounting-framework changes. For each change, document the source, publication date, effective date, personnel and transactions affected, required process adjustments, revised forms and any necessary training. Reassess per diem rates, eligible expenditure categories, approval limits, deadlines, tax treatment and accounting procedures only where the change actually affects them. Test revised calculations and workflows before release, obtain authorized approval, communicate the changes and maintain the superseded version in the controlled archive. Claims already in progress must be assessed under the applicable transition rules rather than automatically recalculated using the newest schedule. Unverified requirements should be identified as pending confirmation and should not be represented as approved organizational policy.

Study instruction:
- "Select one policy change and trace its source, impact assessment, testing, approval, communication and effective date through the controlled SOP history."

Key point:
- Changes must have an authoritative basis, approved effective date and traceable implementation.
- Superseded procedures should remain available for historical review.

Source links:
- United Nations Policy Portal — Travel and relocation expenses
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger procedures and controls
39. Q: What should Finance do when the expense report, bank records and general ledger do not agree?
A: Show answer
Stop the affected settlement from being certified as complete and identify the exact difference. Compare the approved claim with the advance payment, direct supplier payments, refund receipts, reimbursement transactions, foreign-exchange adjustments and journal postings. Check for duplicate lines, missing bank receipts, transactions posted to the wrong trip or project, date cut-off differences, calculation errors and unrecorded corrections. Verify the original evidence rather than forcing totals to agree by changing a balancing figure. Document the amount, cause, corrective action, responsible person, reviewer and evidence of resolution. If the difference cannot be resolved before closing, assess its materiality and escalate it under the approved close procedure. Correcting the accounting record should not erase the original transaction or eliminate the audit trail.

Study instruction:
- "Investigate a settlement mismatch from the source report through bank transactions and journal entries, then document the approved correction."

Key point:
- Reconciliation differences must be explained and supported before final certification.
- Corrections need evidence, approval and a retained audit trail.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 13 — Receivables and account balances
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger reconciliations
40. Q: How should an NGO decide whether an unresolved travel advance requires recovery, an accounting correction or a write-off?
A: Show answer
Determine what the balance represents before choosing the treatment. An amount may relate to an unsubmitted claim, unspent cash, an ineligible expense, a payment error, a refund not yet received, an accounting misclassification or a disputed recovery. Each circumstance requires a different investigation. Finance should obtain the original approval, advance payment, actual travel evidence, claim history, communications and relevant policy. Recovery is appropriate only through an authorized process supported by the facts and applicable rules. An accounting correction addresses an inaccurate classification or posting, while a write-off is a separate decision requiring the organization's approval, documented justification and any necessary legal or donor review. None of these actions should be used simply to eliminate an aged balance. Any residual impact must be reflected consistently in the ledger, management report and supporting records.

Study instruction:
- "Classify several unresolved advance balances by cause and justify whether each requires claim completion, cash recovery, correction or a separately authorized write-off."

Key point:
- The resolution must match the actual nature of the outstanding balance.
- Recovery, correction and write-off are distinct actions with separate evidence and approval requirements.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 13 — Receivables and recoverable amounts
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Journal entries and financial controls
41. Q: How can an NGO test whether its travel advance SOP works in practice before approving it for organization-wide use?
A: Show answer
Conduct an end-to-end walkthrough using a completed trip and at least one exception case. Verify that the written steps match the actual approval workflow, payment process, travel evidence, settlement calculation and ledger postings. Test a case where the traveller spends less than the advance, a case where additional reimbursement is due, an overdue claim, a trip cancellation and a claim with missing documents. Compare the expected and actual outcomes, including the treatment of residual balances and the time required to resolve them. Record process gaps, control failures, unclear responsibilities and policy-dependent decisions. Assign each required correction to an owner with a due date and retest the revised process. The SOP should not be marked approved merely because the document is complete; adoption requires authorized sign-off and evidence that the process is workable under actual organizational conditions.

Study instruction:
- "Perform a documented walkthrough, retain sample evidence and test each identified corrective action before recommending SOP adoption."

Key point:
- A complete document is not proof that its procedures operate effectively.
- Approval should follow testing, resolution of critical gaps and authorized sign-off.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 6 — Travel Management
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Financial controls and transaction workflows

Table 6
Cash Imprest and Field-Site Cash Advance Controls
1. Q: What is the purpose of an NGO cash imprest and field-site cash advance SOP?
A: Show answer
The purpose is to protect organizational cash, ensure that payments are authorized and legitimate, maintain accountable custody, record cash movements promptly, reconcile balances, settle advances within approved deadlines, and preserve evidence for accounting, donor reporting, and audit. The SOP must distinguish a fixed imprest fund from a temporary advance issued to a named individual for a specific activity. It should establish responsibilities, approval limits, documentation requirements, security arrangements, exception handling, reporting, and closure procedures. The supplied example identifies itself as a controlled draft and uses fictional organizational details; it must not be treated as an adopted policy. Before implementation, the organization must confirm its actual legal entity, accounting framework, jurisdiction, internal policies, delegations, donor conditions, and site-security requirements.

Study instruction:
- "Identify the purpose, scope, control objectives, and adoption requirements of the example SOP before applying any procedure."
Key point:
- Cash accountability requires authorization, custody, evidence, reconciliation, and independent review.
- A draft or fictional example does not authorize real transactions.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 12 — Cash Management
2. Q: What is the difference between a fixed imprest fund and a temporary field-site cash advance?
A: Show answer
A fixed imprest fund is an authorized amount of cash maintained for recurring eligible operational payments. Under an approved imprest arrangement, replenishment is generally based on qualifying expenditures that have been supported, approved, and not previously reimbursed, subject to the organization's adopted policy. A temporary field-site advance is issued to a named recipient for a defined purpose, amount, currency, and settlement deadline. The recipient must subsequently account for eligible expenditure and return unused cash. The balances therefore require different reconciliation methods. An imprest fund is reconciled against its authorized float, while an advance is reconciled from the amount issued to accepted eligible expenditure, returned cash, authorized adjustments, and any unresolved residual. A temporary advance must never be treated as a fixed imprest merely because both arrangements involve physical cash.

Study instruction:
- "Compare the purpose, accounting records, reconciliation equation, replenishment process, and closure conditions for both arrangements."
Key point:
- Fixed imprest replenishment and individual advance settlement are different processes.
- Use the accounting treatment approved for each arrangement.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 14 — Accounts Payable and Petty Cash Fund Procedures
3. Q: When should an NGO establish a cash imprest or permit field-site cash advances?
A: Show answer
Cash should be maintained only where there is a documented operational need and a justified reason why an electronic payment, direct supplier payment, controlled bank transfer, or other safer non-cash method is unsuitable. The assessment should consider the urgency of the activity, supplier availability, site connectivity, banking access, transaction frequency, transaction value, security exposure, and the cost of maintaining and transporting cash. The fund owner should document the expected use, estimate the required amount, identify the accountable custodian, assess the location, and obtain approval under the applicable delegation. Recurring operational needs may support a fixed imprest arrangement; a defined one-off activity may justify a temporary advance. Convenience or historic practice alone is insufficient justification. If cash introduces unacceptable safety or control risks, the transaction should be redesigned or held until a suitable arrangement is approved.

Study instruction:
- "Evaluate two proposed field payments and document why cash or a non-cash method is appropriate for each."
Key point:
- Cash is an operational method, not an automatic payment preference.
- Authorization must follow documented need and risk assessment.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 14 — Petty Cash Fund Procedures
4. Q: What must be verified before adapting the example SOP to an actual NGO?
A: Show answer
Begin by confirming the organization's registered legal entity, jurisdiction, reporting purpose, applicable accounting framework, functional currency, fiscal period, approved finance manual, delegation of authority, procurement and payment policies, grant agreements, and applicable statutory requirements. Verify current site-security assessments, authorized storage arrangements, cash movement procedures, evidence-retention requirements, and the technical capabilities of the accounting system. Each source or policy must be recorded with its identifier, current version, effective date, applicability, responsible reviewer, and approval status. The example's Indonesian starting point does not establish that a particular Indonesian law or accounting framework applies to every entity. Likewise, references to UN procedures do not make UN-specific thresholds or Umoja workflows mandatory for another NGO. Material unresolved matters must remain on hold until the competent owner confirms and approves them.

Study instruction:
- "Complete an entity and policy applicability checklist and identify every requirement that remains unverified."
Key point:
- Authority, jurisdiction, and actual organizational circumstances determine applicability.
- Unverified limits, delegations, and safety arrangements must not be treated as approved.

- IAI SAK Update and Standards Endorsement Index
5. Q: What information should be recorded in the cash fund master register?
A: Show answer
Every fund should have a unique FundID and a controlled master record containing the approved purpose, entity, site, currency, authorized float, applicable transaction limits, effective dates, named custodian, alternate custodian, fund owner, approving authority, security classification, accounting mapping, and record locations. Record the supporting approval reference and the date on which each parameter was authorized. Changes to the amount, location, currency, custodian, or permitted use must be reviewed before becoming effective. Maintain an append-only history so reviewers can distinguish the settings that applied at the transaction date from later changes. The master must also record whether the fund is requested, awaiting evidence, under review, approved for future activation, active, suspended, pending closure, closed, or rejected. An incomplete or expired master record should prevent activation or use where the missing information affects authorization or safety.

Study instruction:
- "Design a fund master record and test whether a payment can be traced to the correct FundID, site, currency, custodian, and effective approval."
Key point:
- Fund identity and approved parameters must be effective-dated and traceable.
- Master-data completeness is a prerequisite for reliable cash control.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 12 — Cash Management
6. Q: How should authorized float amounts, transaction limits, and maximum site cash exposure be established?
A: Show answer
The organization should define each limit separately because the limits serve different purposes. The authorized float determines the approved size of a fixed imprest fund. A single-transaction limit controls an individual payment; a daily or cumulative limit controls aggregate expenditure; a maximum-holding limit controls physical cash exposure; and a transport limit governs approved movement of cash. Any emergency authority must have a defined scope, amount, location, duration, and approving role. Finance should calculate proposed limits from documented operating needs and current security assessments, while the authorized approver confirms the delegation and effective dates. The SOP's placeholders are intentionally unapproved and must not be replaced with convenient estimates. The organization must also prohibit transaction splitting to evade limits and must review whether a payment breaches more than one limit. A failure in any mandatory limit check should stop or hold the transaction.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a limit register showing each parameter's business purpose, calculation basis, approval, effective date, and failure response."
Key point:
- Float, transaction, cumulative, holding, and transport limits are distinct controls.
- Limit changes require documented approval and testing.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 14 — Petty Cash and Imprest Account Management
7. Q: How should responsibilities be divided between the requester, fund owner, custodian, approver, reviewer, and accountant?
A: Show answer
The requester establishes the business need and provides supporting information. The fund or budget owner verifies purpose and funding availability. The custodian safeguards the cash and records each movement. An authorized approver confirms the transaction is within the relevant delegation. An independent reviewer examines the evidence, reconciliation, or settlement, while the general-ledger owner is responsible for appropriate posting and financial reporting. A security or incident owner handles relevant safety events, and the records owner maintains controlled evidence. Wherever practicable, the person preparing a payment or reconciliation must not be its sole approver or independent reviewer. If staffing limitations prevent full separation, management must implement documented compensating controls, such as an independent post-payment review and a separate cash count. Responsibilities and substitutions must be formally assigned rather than inferred from job titles or shared system access.

Study instruction:
- "Create a RACI matrix for fund establishment, disbursement, reconciliation, replenishment, settlement, posting, and closure."
Key point:
- Segregation of duties reduces the risk of unauthorized transactions and concealed errors.
- Any compensating control must be independently performed and evidenced.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 12 — Cash Management
8. Q: How should a custodian and an alternate custodian be appointed and controlled?
A: Show answer
Appointment should follow documented authorization that identifies the individual, fund, site, currency, responsibilities, effective dates, applicable cash limits, and permitted access. The custodian should acknowledge responsibility for the amount received and demonstrate understanding of payment, documentation, reconciliation, security, and incident procedures. An alternate should be formally approved rather than informally allowed to use the fund. Access rights, keys, combinations, and other secrets must be handled through approved secure arrangements and must not be entered into broadly accessible cash registers. When a custodian leaves, changes roles, takes extended leave, or is replaced, perform a documented count, reconcile outstanding transactions, list open advances and exceptions, transfer custody formally, and update access. Unexplained balances must remain open for investigation rather than being transferred as if they were verified cash.

Study instruction:
- "Review a custodian appointment and handover package and verify that responsibility, cash, open items, and access were transferred."
Key point:
- Custody must be assigned to named, authorized people for defined periods.
- Handover requires evidence of physical cash and outstanding accountability.
Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 12 — Cash Management
9. Q: What controls should govern physical cash storage, keys, access, and site security?
A: Show answer
Each site must have a current risk assessment and an approved storage and access arrangement appropriate to the actual environment. The records should identify the accountable custodian, approved storage reference, access roles, effective dates, key-control arrangements, and required handover procedures. They must not expose combinations, passwords, detailed safe locations, or sensitive cash-transfer routes to unauthorized readers. Shared generic access should be avoided because it prevents reliable attribution of custody and activity. Management must consider theft, robbery, evacuation, natural disasters, political instability, communications outages, and the safety of employees responsible for cash. Security arrangements should be reviewed after material site or risk changes. If a planned payment or transfer cannot be made safely, the transaction must be suspended and referred through the approved security and finance escalation process. Cash or property must never be defended through unsafe confrontation.

Study instruction:
- "Evaluate a site cash-storage assessment and identify risks, access gaps, responsible owners, and required corrective actions."
Key point:
- Personal safety takes precedence over cash recovery.
- Storage, access, and movement controls must reflect current site risks.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 12 — Cash Management
10. Q: What checks must be completed before any cash disbursement is made?
A: Show answer
The payment should proceed only after confirming that the fund is active and effective, the site and currency match, the amount is within every applicable limit, the purpose and payee are permitted, and an authorized person has approved the transaction in advance. Verify budget, project, donor or grant conditions, procurement requirements, duplicate claims, transaction splitting, prior replenishment, required evidence, payee acknowledgement, accounting coding, and relevant tax considerations. Confirm that custody, security, and offline procedures are current and that a valid voucher or movement reference is available. An expired approval, unsupported purpose, unrecognized payee, exceeded limit, or unresolved duplicate should trigger a documented hold or rejection. A receipt obtained after payment cannot replace approval that was required before payment. Emergency exceptions must follow specific, authorized procedures rather than informal verbal permission.

Study instruction:
- "Use a pre-payment checklist to test a routine purchase, a high-value request, a duplicate claim, and an expired authorization."
Key point:
- Payment approval must precede disbursement and cover the actual transaction.
- Failed mandatory checks require a documented hold, return, or rejection.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 14 — Accounts Payable and Petty Cash Fund Procedures
11. Q: What information should a disbursement request contain before it is submitted for approval?
A: Show answer
A request should identify the requester, proposed payee, purpose, amount, currency, expected payment date, fund and site, project or cost centre, budget or funding reference, and the reason cash is required. It should include available quotations, invoices, contracts, activity approvals, procurement evidence, or other documents appropriate to the transaction. The request should show whether the expense is recurring, urgent, donor-funded, subject to special eligibility conditions, or potentially restricted. The approver must be able to determine the business purpose, authorization, limit compliance, available funding, and evidence requirements without reconstructing the request from multiple informal messages. Missing material information should be returned for completion or held for authorized review. The final approval reference must be linked to the payment voucher and subsequent accounting record.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a sample cash disbursement request and verify that another reviewer can independently assess its purpose, authority, limits, and supporting evidence."
Key point:
- The request establishes the business case and authorization for payment.
- Supporting documents must connect the approval to the transaction actually made.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 14 — Accounts Payable and Petty Cash Fund Procedures
12. Q: How should payees, prohibited transactions, and potential conflicts of interest be checked?
A: Show answer
Verify the identity of the intended recipient using evidence appropriate to the payment and applicable organizational procedures. Confirm that the recipient, transaction, purpose, amount, and payment method comply with the approved fund rules and relevant procurement or funding conditions. Personal use, informal loans, unsupported IOUs, self-approval, unauthorized purposes, unapproved transfers, and payments designed to circumvent limits should be prohibited or separately controlled. A payment to the custodian or requester requires particular scrutiny and must not be authorized solely by that individual. Screen potential duplicate claims and compare the payee, amount, date, purpose, invoice or receipt reference, and prior replenishment or settlement status. Suspected conflicts or irregularities must be referred to an independent reviewer. The control should prevent unauthorized activity without assuming that a name or account match alone proves fraud.

Study instruction:
- "Test a sample of payees and transactions for identity, authority, prohibited purposes, duplicates, and self-approval."
Key point:
- Payee identity and transaction legitimacy must be independently supportable.
- Self-approval and limit circumvention must not be accepted as routine exceptions.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 14 — Accounts Payable and Payment Controls
13. Q: What supporting evidence should exist for each cash movement?
A: Show answer
Each movement should be supported by a unique voucher or transaction reference, approved request, payment date and time, payee, purpose, amount and currency, authorization reference, evidence of cash receipt, and appropriate receipt or invoice. Depending on the transaction, additional evidence may include procurement records, proof of service or goods received, activity reports, travel documentation, budget approval, tax support, and donor eligibility checks. The record should identify the source file, document location, reviewer, posting reference, and any subsequent correction or exception. Evidence must be sufficient to demonstrate that the payment was authorized, actually occurred, served a legitimate purpose, and was properly classified. Missing evidence should be recorded as an exception and investigated. A handwritten explanation or manager statement alone is not equivalent to independently corroborated evidence in every circumstance.

Study instruction:
- "Select cash vouchers and trace each from the approved request through payment acknowledgement, supporting evidence, and accounting entry."
Key point:
- Every cash movement needs an identifiable transaction record and appropriate evidence.
- Incomplete evidence must remain visible until an authorized disposition is documented.
